**2026-11-21 — review A28 (Codex Astra, low effort, of `ebf460e..2977264`: the cross-check of the tuple's kept hash and of A27's fixes).** Six findings (RL-622 to RL-627), each run on both sides before its verdict, all confirmed.
* **Fixed, three:** the harness's mask writes `<cases>` for the cases' directory in a warning-shaped line, where it took the directory off, so a line with the bare name is no longer the same line (RL-622; C-8-19's expectation recorded again; the specification's harness sentence says so, inside v0.26); an unclosed file's ResourceWarning at the program's end calls the warning function the runtime kept, as RL-615 made it elsewhere (RL-625); the program that tells a first store that fails from the look before it is in the tests (RL-627).
* **Open, two, each a step of its own:** the runtime's own recursion has no guard on the native stack, so hashing a tuple nested far deeper than the stack ends the program where CPython raises RecursionError (RL-624, older, first in TODO); a starred tuple reaches a callable written in C as itself in CPython and as a new tuple here, which an `lru_cache` key, a subclassed exception's `args` and `itemgetter` show (RL-626).
* **For the author, a wording (RL-623):** a tuple of constants is one object in CPython and made each time here; decision 1 accepts identity differences, and D-03's row does not name this one.
* **The reviewer's saved outputs, compared pairwise:** nothing beyond the report (two stops that name themselves, D-20's tracemalloc line).
* **Held by:** `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_21_a28.py`, the harness's test.

**2026-11-21 — a type's slots are read without its metaclass (RL-621, the class behind review A27's RL-616; no decision of the author's was needed).** Where CPython's C asks whether an argument is an index, a buffer, a sequence or a number, it reads a slot of the argument's type. The preludes that stand for those functions asked `hasattr(type(x), "__index__")`, an attribute lookup on the class, which asks the metaclass.
* **What a program sees:** CPython's behaviour for an argument whose metaclass has a `__getattr__` or a property of a special name: the metaclass is not asked; a type without the slot is refused with CPython's `TypeError` (for a buffer the prelude had stopped by name, `[P2+] the buffer protocol`, on the metaclass's word), and a type with the slot is used as one.
* **How:** `_has_slot(value, name)` in each of the 22 preludes, which reads the dicts of the classes of the type's MRO, as `_type_lookup` does in `_signal`, `posix` and `_functools`.
* **Held by:** the sweep `pipeline/tests/test_slot_checks.py`: 37 functions, each with the two arguments, a process for each of 74 rows, compiled against CPython; 53 rows differed before the change.

**2026-11-21 — data deeper than the native stack (RL-624 of review A28, case C-8-20; no decision of the author's was needed for what CPython does itself).** The review found that hashing a tuple nested 300 000 deep ended the program. Measured, the class was wider and nearer: under the default stack of 8 MiB a compiled program ended with SIGSEGV when it let go of a chain of 30 000 linked objects or dicts, or of 60 000 nested tuples or lists, and when it took the repr or the str of such data or compared two. CPython lets go of a million and raises RecursionError for the walks.
* **What a program sees:** a chain of any length is let go, every object dying once, a weak reference to it dead. The hash of a tuple of tuples, the repr and the str of nested containers and a comparison raise RecursionError where the data is deeper than the stack, and the program goes on with the data as it was. Below the depth at which the stack is short nothing changes: the order of deaths is the nesting's. Past it the order is the machine's, in CPython as here.
* **How:** CPython's own way since 3.14, the stack pointer against bounds read at the start. `docs/design.md`, the paragraph "The native stack"; the model first (`Heap.stack_room`, `_dealloc`), the header in lockstep with it.
* **For the author, a wording:** a program that raises its recursion limit past what the native stack holds now gets a RecursionError at the stack, where it ended on the guard page before; CPython's pure-Python recursion goes on, its frames being on the heap. That is D-17's matter (a compiled call is a native call), and its row does not say it yet.
* **Held by:** C-8-20 on the compiled leg (the program starts itself again under a stack of 1 MiB: six chains let go, twelve walks that raise); `test_a_chain_longer_than_the_native_stack` in the model's tests; part 9 of the lockstep test of the state (`make object-test`). An allocation-heavy loop takes the same time as before (15.4 s both).

**2026-11-21 — the item iterators of dict and OrderedDict keep their own pair (RL-584 of review A25; no decision of the author's was needed).** CPython's item iterators have a result tuple of their own (`di_result`) and give it again while nothing else holds it, as `enumerate` and `zip` do (RL-551). A compiled program made a new pair each time, so the last pair's key and value died as soon as the program let go of them, where under CPython the iterator still holds them until its next step or its end.
* **What a program sees:** the value of the pair an iterator gave last stays alive in the iterator after the mapping lets go of it, and goes at the next step, the old key before the old value; a pair the program keeps is the program's, and the iterator makes another. For a dict's items, reversed items and an OrderedDict's.
* **How:** `Iterator::result` and the `pair` step in `runtime/src/ops.cpp`; `_f_result` in the prelude's `odict_iterator`, whose `__reduce__` shares the one reference with its copy as the C's struct copy does.
* **Held by:** the program RL-584 in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_21.py` (both mappings, forward and reversed, the hash of a pair used again, the reduced iterator). Checked: the sweeps of the order of release and of the state, the tests about dicts, collections and iterators, three cases compiled, lint, the six compiled targets.

**2026-11-21 — what `sys.modules` holds before a program's first statement: a registered deviation (the author's decision: A, with C deferred; RL-604 of review A26, D-30, spec v0.27).** CPython's start imports modules that a program can then find in `sys.modules` without importing them: 32 at the first statement, 20 under `-S`. A compiled program has 15, the ones the runtime's own start needs, in the runtime's order, so `"os" in sys.modules` is False and `sys.modules["posix"]` is a KeyError before any import. Nothing said so. The author was given three ways (a deviation that says it; the start's modules as `python -S` has them; as `python` has them) and chose the first, with the third "as an item deferred for later re-consideration".
* **What a program sees:** as before. A module is in `sys.modules` once the program, or a module it imports, has imported it.
* **Wording added to the specification (v0.27).** The Environment paragraph: "Before a program's first statement `sys.modules` holds the modules the runtime's own start imported, which are not those of CPython's start (D-30); a module is there once the program, or a module it imports, has imported it." The register's row D-30, with the two lists, and its note.
* **Deferred:** the start's modules as CPython has them, listed in TODO under Later with what it would take.

**2026-11-21 — review A29 (Codex Astra, low effort, of `2977264..b04274c`: the cross-check of the native stack, of the sweep of a type's slots and of A28's fixes).** Ten findings (RL-628 to RL-637), each run on both sides before its verdict, all confirmed; none ends a program, and the reviewer's rows for chains let go and for walks of data deeper than the stack agreed.
* **The recursion limit counts the frames CPython has (RL-632, RL-633, RL-637).** A compiled program has frames CPython does not have: the helper of a list, set or dict comprehension, which CPython runs in the frame around it, and the functions of the preludes, which stand for C functions. They were counted toward `sys.getrecursionlimit()`, so a recursion through a comprehension, `map`, `sorted`, a property or `functools.reduce` reached half CPython's depth, and the repr of a list nested 100 deep raised RecursionError under a limit of 50. They are not counted now; they take the look at the native stack that RL-624's step added. The sweep of 53 shapes of a level agrees with CPython on 52.
* **The 53rd is CPython's own (RL-639, open, for D-17's wording):** a class called with a Python `__init__` takes two entries a level under CPython, whose specialised instruction pushes a frame beside `__init__`'s, and one on the first calls of a site.
* **Special methods are the type's, bound (RL-628 to RL-631, RL-635, RL-636, RL-638).** Seven more places where a prelude read a special name off the class as an attribute, which asks the metaclass, or called what it found with the object: csv's number check, `math.ceil`, `floor` and `trunc`, `operator.length_hint`, `_locale.bindtextdomain`, the namespace of a class statement, `Counter`'s fast path, the format of a type variable's evaluator. `_lookup_special` is in every prelude now.
* **The harness (RL-634):** a run whose standard error has the mask's own `<cases>/` fails.
* **Held by:** `pipeline/tests/test_recursion_counts.py` (the sweep), `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_21_a29.py` (seven programs), the harness's tests.

**2026-11-21 — review A30 (Codex Astra, low effort, of `b04274c..6ff3049`: the cross-check of the recursion counts, of the special methods of A29's fixes, of the item iterators' pair and of the native stack).** Twelve findings (RL-643 to RL-654), each run on both sides before its verdict, all confirmed; none ends a program, and the reviewer's 112 runs of data deeper than the stack and its 40 rows of recursion counts agreed.
* **A type's slots are the type's, whatever its metaclass shows (RL-644; the class is done).** RL-621 had the preludes read a slot by walking `type(value).__mro__` and `vars(klass)`. Both are attributes of a class, so a metaclass with a property named `__mro__` or `__dict__` answered first: `os.fspath(C())` called another class's `__fspath__`. The walk is now the runtime's own, read from the type as `_PyType_Lookup` reads it, and every prelude has it as `_has_slot(value, name)` and `_type_lookup(value, name, absent)` beside `_lookup_special`; the 25 copies written in Python and five other walks are gone. Held by a test that reads the preludes for such a walk.
* **An exception the runtime caught and sent on keeps its traceback (RL-645; the class is done).** The runtime marks the exception in flight so that each frame it leaves records itself. Where the runtime caught one, did work that raised and caught another inside (an optional lookup), and then threw the first on, the second had taken the mark with it: a RecursionError that passed 60 `__set_name__` calls arrived with three entries. `rethrow(e)` says again which exception is in flight, and it was used in four places; it is now used in all 54 catch blocks that name their exception and send it on. Held by a test that reads the runtime's sources for a bare `throw;` in such a block.
* **An error that was pending while the C did something goes on as it is (RL-650).** Where CPython's C clears a field or lets a reference go with an error set and then returns, a prelude now catches the exception, does that work after its handler has ended, and calls `_raise_as_is(exc)`, which every prelude has: the same exception goes on with the context it had and no entry of the prelude's. An OrderedDict's iterator uses it: after a key's `__hash__` failed it is at its end, as CPython's is.
* **The others:** a copy of a dict's item iterator walks with the iterator's own pair (RL-643, which RL-584 had done for OrderedDict only); `operator.length_hint` and a weak proxy's `next` test a slot without binding it (RL-646, RL-647); the path conversions take a `__fspath__` that cannot be bound, or is None, for no path and raise their own TypeError (RL-648, RL-649); `io.FileIO` and `open` test their name's type, and a name whose conversion to an int fails is a path (RL-651); a `fileno` that is None is called (RL-652); `RawIOBase.readall` goes on after EINTR (RL-654).
* **No change:** the words of the TypeError for a `read` that is None (RL-653, D-08).
* **Left open, a step of its own each:** the other calls of `isinstance` in the preludes where the C tests the object's type (RL-655, 184 calls in 20 preludes), and the eight other places where `_io` goes on after EINTR (RL-656).
* **Held by:** `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_21_a30.py` (nine programs, two tests that read the sources).

**2026-11-21 — `_io` goes on after an interrupted call wherever CPython's does (RL-656, the class behind RL-654; no decision of the author's was needed).** CPython's `_io` calls a stream's method again when it raised an OSError whose errno is EINTR, at nine places; the prelude did at one since RL-654.
* **What a program sees:** a `read`, `peek`, `readinto` or `write` of a program's own stream class that raises `InterruptedError` is called again by `IOBase.readline` and `writelines`, by a buffered reader or writer over it, and by a `TextIOWrapper`'s write, `read(n)` and `readline`, and what was read or written so far is kept. Any other error goes through as before. `TextIOWrapper.read()` without a size does not retry, in CPython either.
* **Found with it:** `IOBase.readline` reads the stream's `peek` attribute once, not for every chunk.
* **Held by:** the program RL-656 in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_21_a30.py` (75 rows; 22 differed before).

**2026-11-21 — a type test of the C's does not ask an object's `__class__` (RL-655, the class behind RL-651; no decision of the author's was needed).** `isinstance(x, str)` is true for an object whose `__class__` property says str, and reads that property; CPython's C asks `PyUnicode_Check(x)`, which reads the object's type. The preludes that stand for C asked `isinstance` in 169 places.
* **What a program sees:** an object that only says it is a str, an int, a tuple or a dict is refused by the library's functions as CPython refuses it, and its `__class__` is not read. Before, 151 of 191 calls tried differed: such an object was written into `json.dumps`'s text and taken as `print`'s separator, `_imp.is_builtin` answered for it, and `codecs.utf_8_encode` ended the program with a segmentation fault.
* **How:** `issubclass(type(x), T)` in 166 places. Three calls stay as they are, because CPython asks `PyObject_IsInstance` there too: `deque + other`, the class pattern of a match statement, and the stand-in CPython itself runs in `iterating.py`. The two files of importlib's own Python are CPython's text and keep theirs.
* **Found with it:** a buffered writer whose raw `write` returns what is no number raises CPython's OSError (`raw write() returned invalid length -1`), set over the conversion's error.
* **Held by:** the sweep `pipeline/tests/test_type_checks.py`, a process for each of 191 rows, compiled against CPython, and a test that reads the preludes for an `isinstance` call.

**2026-11-21, night — five of the open items of review A25 (RL-585, RL-593, RL-594, RL-597, RL-598; no decision of the author's was needed).**
* **A `__del__` is bound as a special method is (RL-597).** CPython's `slot_tp_finalize` looks the finaliser up on the type and binds it: a function, or a method of a built-in type, is called with the object; a staticmethod, a classmethod or another descriptor is bound by its `__get__` and called with nothing, as is a builtin function or a callable instance, which bind nothing. The runtime called whatever the class had with the object, so a staticmethod `__del__` was an unraisable TypeError and never ran. A `__del__` that is None is an unraisable TypeError, as under CPython, and a binding that raises leaves nothing to call and nothing reported.
* **An exact BufferedRWPair, BytesIO or StringIO has no finaliser (RL-585, RL-598).** Their C deallocs call none, so a `close` kept on the instance is not called at its end and a pair's weak references go before its reader and writer close. A subclass of the program's has IOBase's finaliser, as before.
* **A struct sequence lets go of its fields first one first (RL-593)**, the fields past the visible ones after them, as `structseq_dealloc` does; an ordinary tuple goes last one first.
* **A Context's iterator holds the mapping until it dies (RL-594)**, exhausted or not.
* **Left open of that review:** RL-583 (with memoryview), RL-588 (slot names sorted as objects: the order of slots is the model's), RL-591, RL-592, RL-599.
* **Held by:** the programs RL-585, RL-593, RL-594 and RL-597 in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_21.py`. Checked: `make object-test` (the header against the model), the release-order and state sweeps, the weak reference and io tests, the six compiled targets.

**2026-11-21, night — review A31 (Codex Astra, low effort, of `30cd727..ad307d1`), and what a prelude reads of a subclass's instance (RL-657 to RL-698; no decision of the author's was needed for the 39 fixed).** The session asked the reviewer for the neighbour of RL-655: an argument that really is an instance of a subclass of str, int, bytes, tuple, list or dict, whose class overrides methods. 41 findings, each run on both sides before its verdict.
* **The class (RL-698, 32 findings in 16 preludes).** Once CPython's C has tested an argument's type it reads the object's own data through the concrete API, which calls nothing of the class's. A prelude that wrote `len(x)`, `x[i]`, `x == y`, `i in x`, `for c in x`, `bytes(x)` or `int(x)` on the argument reached the subclass's override. Four results were wrong data: `hashlib.sha256` of a bytes subclass with a `__bytes__` was the digest of other bytes, `hmac.compare_digest` was true for unequal bytes, `json.dumps` wrote another key and value for a pair with a `__getitem__`, `struct.pack` packed another byte.
* **How:** a prelude takes the data once. `_own_data(x)` gives, for an instance of a subclass of str, bytes, int, float or tuple, the exact base object with the same data; `_buffer_bytes(x)` the octets of a bytes or a bytearray as an exact bytes. Both are the runtime's and every prelude has them. A list, a dict and a bytearray, which the C reads live, are read by their base type's own methods at each turn. `docs/design.md` has the rule beside the type tests'.
* **What stays a call, because CPython's C makes it too:** a dict subclass's `items()` in `json.dumps`, a list subclass's `__iter__` where the C lists a non-exact sequence, a str subclass's `__hash__` as the key of `struct`'s cache, an int subclass's `__int__` in `TextIOWrapper.seek`, a separator's `__len__` in `binascii.hexlify`, `replace` of the text in a translating `TextIOWrapper.write`.
* **Beside the class:** `repr` of a bytes or bytearray subclass calls its own `__repr__` (RL-696); the GeneratorExit of `close()` has the exception the caller is handling as its context (RL-678); an argv whose length cannot be taken is an empty argv (RL-697); a UnicodeError's start and end are plain ints, as the C's struct has them.
* **Open:** importlib's frames in a traceback through `importlib.import_module` (RL-668: the import system's frames are Python frames in CPython, a step of its own, with the recursion count of RL-639), and the lifetime of an argument that is a temporary of the call (RL-695, for the author).
* **Held by:** `pipeline/tests/test_subclass_data.py`: the reviewer's driver as a sweep (462 calls in two modes, a process each: 244 of 924 runs differed before), its 36 reduced programs, and five programs for the findings beside the class.
* **The reviewer's saved outputs, compared pairwise and run again on the fixed build (5 409 pairs, 645 differing as saved):** nothing beyond the report. What still differs is the functions that stop by name, `hashlib.new` and its name without OpenSSL (D-20), `fcntl`, which the closure omits, the traceback through `import_module` (RL-668) and the 46 rows of the ownership driver that let go of their input inside the callee (RL-695: with the input made inside the function all 50 agree).

**2026-11-22 — the names of `__slots__` are sorted as objects (RL-588 of review A25; no decision of the author's was needed).** CPython's `type_new` sorts the slot names with `PyList_Sort`. For plain strs that is their order by code point, which the model and the header had; for a name that is an instance of a str subclass the class's own `__lt__` is asked, and what the sort leaves is the order of the members in the class's dict and of the slots' release.
* **What a program sees:** the comparisons CPython makes, in its order (none at all where the sort compares the other way round), the members in the sorted order, an object's slots let go in it, and a comparison that raises ends the class statement with that exception.

CPython's `update_one_slot` keeps `slot_tp_new` when a `__new__` is deleted again (it finds a built-in's wrapper and leaves the slot as it is), sets it for a `__new__` anywhere in the MRO, and leaves object's allocator when `object.__new__` itself is stored. So the slot is a bit of the class (`slot_tp_new`), set when the class is made and at each store or delete of the name in it or in a base, never taken back; `object.__init__`'s and `object.__new__`'s rules for excess arguments, `type_call`, `list.__init__`'s rule for keywords and OSError's choice between its `__new__` and `__init__` read it. 22 rows measured against CPython hold it.

* **The sequence protocol is not `len()`, `+` and `.index` (RL-809, RL-814, RL-820, RL-839).** `PySequence_Size` refuses a dict, which `len()` takes: `_sequence_size(x)` is the runtime's (a class of the program's with `__len__` has the slot; a dict, a frozendict, a mappingproxy and a Context have a mapping's length alone), and ast's four uses and `TypeVar`'s substitution take it; `PySequence_Concat` is the primitive `operator.concat` already had; `PySequence_Index` is a loop that compares.

Measured with `dis` for twenty shapes; the rule is in the log's row and the TODO. It is the next step, with a case first.

Of the 14 left, 7 stop by name, 6 are RL-825, and 2 hold a cycle that CPython's collector finalises at exit (D-04, D-07). The 1,265 rows that agreed were run again after the fixes, twice, and agree; the first rerun of the differing rows found three things no finding named (`list.__init__`'s keywords and OSError after a `__new__` was deleted, a None buffer, the struct iterator), which are fixed with the rest.

**2026-11-22 — the order of a display's stores (RL-825 of review A36, case C-3-79; no decision of the author's was needed).** `a, b = X(), Y()` in a function let go of the old `a` before the old `b`; CPython does it the other way round, because its optimizer removes the `SWAP` it made of `BUILD_TUPLE 2, UNPACK_SEQUENCE 2` by exchanging the two `STORE_FAST` (`apply_static_swaps`).
* **The rule**, measured on 3.15.2 for twenty-three shapes: one target (a tuple or a list) of two or three different names on one line, a tuple display of as many items with no star, and every name a fast local of its function. Then the stores are made last name first (`b, a`; `c, b, a`). Everything else stores in the order written: four names, a list display on the right, a nested or starred target, a chained assignment, a cell, a global, a `nonlocal` or an attribute among the targets, targets on two lines, module and class level. A display on two lines is still exchanged; so are constants on the right, parameters as targets, and the statement inside a generator.
* **Where it is:** the shape is seen by the frontend on the `ast` node (`L.Assign.swapped`); the names' kinds are level 2's, where the lowering reverses the stores (`co_varnames` without `co_cellvars`, as CPython has them); eval1, which resolves no scopes, asks the same resolver, so both evaluators and the compiled program store in one order.
* **Held by:** C-3-79 on the compiled leg and both evaluators, and six rows of the reviewer's probes (`pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_22_a36.py`).
* **Measured with it and left for a step of its own:** the stores of a `match` statement's captures. Of thirteen shapes run on both sides eleven agree and two differ (`[a, [b, c]]` stores `b, c, a` under CPython and `a, b, c` compiled; `{"x": a, **b}` stores `b, a` and `a, b`): TODO.

Of the 34 left, 22 stop by name (18 at `sys._getframe(1)`, two at `__bases__` and `__class__` assignment, two are RL-852's), 7 differ in a report's text (D-28), 4 are the lifetime of a call's arguments (RL-695, the author's), and one differs at exit only (D-07). The 3,002 rows that agreed were run again after the fixes and agree (six of them, a probe that imports a package beside it, only once the rerun copied the package too).

**2026-11-22 — the order of a pattern's stores (case C-3-80; the TODO item measured with RL-825; no decision of the author's was needed).** A `match` case stored its captures in the order of capture; CPython's order follows the pattern, and two of the thirteen shapes measured in the morning differed (`[a, [b, c]]` is `b, c, a`; `{"x": a, **b}` is `b, a`). The cause is the one of RL-825: the optimizer removes the SWAPs before a run of `STORE_FAST` by exchanging the stores.
* **A port, not a table.** No rule in terms of the pattern's shape held for all shapes (the lines a pattern is written on matter, and which names are fast locals), so the two functions that decide it are ported: the code generator's stack bookkeeping for each kind of pattern, and `swaptimize` with `apply_static_swaps` (`pipeline/l1/captures.py`, about 250 lines). The frontend keeps what CPython's code has before the stores on the statement that stores the names (`L.Assign.captures`); the lowering and eval1 ask the level-2 resolver which names are fast locals and run the optimizer's half. One name is a plain assignment as before; two or more are one statement, which compiles to the same stores in another order (no tuple is made).
* **Checked against the reference interpreter's own code.** `pipeline/tests/test_match_stores.py` generates 1,500 patterns (every kind nested in every other, on one line and on several, guarded or not, the last case or not) and compares the port's order with the order of the STORE instructions `dis` shows, in four scopes (all fast locals, one global, one cell, module level): 6,000 comparisons, 1,222 of them with an order other than the order of capture. The test fails without the exchanges, without `swaptimize`, and without the line rule (each was taken out once). `dis` is used by the test only.
* **The case.** C-3-80: 41 shapes with objects that say when they go; passes compiled and under both evaluators. Among its rows: a mapping on the pattern's second line stores `a, b, c` where the same pattern on one line stores `b, c, a`.
* **Measured beside it and left in TODO:** where a pattern's temporaries are let go of relative to those stores. 8 of 14 shapes differ: CPython drops the tuple of a mapping pattern's keys between or before the stores, and a subject that only the stack holds dies where the last case consumes it (before the stores), where the compiled program lets go of both after the stores.

**2026-11-22 — review A38 (Codex Astra, low effort, of `b414387..7f7c267`: the name counter per unit, A37's fixes).** 16 findings (RL-859 to RL-874), each run on both sides, all confirmed and fixed. Nothing was found on the generated names. No decision of the author's was needed.
* **What a class stores as `__init__` (RL-859, RL-860; both high).** `type_call` handed every built-in the new object as its receiver. CPython's `slot_tp_init` looks the name up as a method: a function, or a method of a built-in type that is not bound, is called with the object first, and a method of another type refuses an object that is not its own (`C.__init__ = list.__init__; C()` is a TypeError; compiled it ran list's initialiser on a C, and on an int subclass ended in a segmentation fault). Anything else is bound by its own `__get__` if it has one: a bound built-in (`sink.append`) and a built-in function have none and are called with the arguments alone. The direct call is kept where the object is of the method's type, so the usual construction costs one pointer comparison more. The class was swept beyond the reviewer's two kinds: 23 kinds of thing stored, 14 base types, three ways of calling, 966 calls, all the same on both sides (`init_kinds`).
* **The allocator slot again (RL-861, RL-863).** RL-841's fix gave `type_call` and `tp_new_is` the slot and left two readers of the name, `list.__init__` and OSError's choice between its `__new__` and its `__init__`; both ask `tp_new_is` now. `_random.Random.__init__` has the same generated rule and refused every keyword; it asks `_tp_new_is`. That closes the TODO item on the clinic's inventory (`frozenset.__new__`, the other one it named, agrees in the reviewer's rows).
* **FileIO's opener (RL-862, high; RL-868, RL-869).** An opener is given the flags with `O_CLOEXEC`, and the descriptor it returns is made non-inheritable whatever the opener did, as `_Py_set_inheritable(fd, 0)` does: before, a descriptor an opener left inheritable went to every child process. The path's bytes and the mode's characters are read without asking a subclass, the message has `%.200s` of the mode (whole characters within 200 bytes), and what the opener returned is let go of once its number is read.
* **After the handler, in five more places (RL-866, RL-870, RL-871, RL-873, and RL-872's `del`).** The EINTR test converted an errno inside the `except` clause at nine places; `_interrupted(failed)` is called after it, and the success path of each place is unchanged. A warning's second search for its file and the file's close, the pieces of a failed `re.sub` before the subject's buffer, and what a buffered stream's raw close returned (let go of with the lock held, so a finaliser that closes again is a reentrant call). `findall` and `split` keep their `finally`: their pieces are exact str or bytes, and no finaliser can see the order.
* **A store and what it lets go of (RL-865, RL-874).** `staticmethod` and `classmethod` copy the names of what the field holds after the store, not of the argument; a newline decoder's readiness flag stands for `errors != NULL` and is never cleared by a second `__init__`.
* **The rest.** A class statement's class over frozendict is a sequence to `PySequence_Concat` and `PySequence_Repeat` (RL-864: `PyDict_Check` does not cover frozendict, and its `__getitem__`, a method descriptor, gives the class `sq_item`); `UnicodeDecodeError.__init__` converts the two indices before it asks the object for its bytes (RL-867).
* **The saved outputs, pairwise.** 490 rows differed, in the main repository as in the reviewer's worktree; all 490 agree now. 125 more stop by name (112 at a subclass of `super`, the others at `range.__new__`, `__bases__` assignment, code attributes and unstaged texts).

**2026-11-22 — Question 28 answered by the author: B (the main thread's stack; D-17 and decision 16).** The question (put 2026-11-21 with RL-624, RL-642): a compiled call is a native call of about 690 bytes and a walk of nested data through a prelude takes 4 to 8 KiB a level, so under the 8 MiB stack of decision 16 a program that raises its recursion limit stops at about 12 000 small calls and `json.dumps` of a list nested 1 500 deep is a RecursionError, where CPython, whose Python frames are on the heap, goes on. The options were A (keep 8 MiB and say so), B (256 MiB by default) and C (1 GiB). The author's words: "q28: B, please note for later that such stack growth might flag a deeper design issue and needs analysis and approach revision at a later stage".
* **Decided.** The program runs on a main stack of 256 MiB by default, reserved as address space and committed only as it is used; `main_stack_mib` in `bella.toml` still overrides it. Measured with `ulimit -s 262144` before the decision: about 390 000 small calls, and `json.dumps` of a list nested 5 000 deep works (50 000 deep is still a RecursionError). Decision 16's "8 MiB main thread" becomes 256 MiB; other threads keep 2 MiB. D-17 then states the bound that remains (a RecursionError where the stack is nearly used, before the limit a program set); its words are put to the author with the implementation.
* **Noted for later, in the author's words.** Such stack growth might flag a deeper design issue and needs analysis and a revision of the approach at a later stage. What the measurements say it would look at: a compiled call's frame on the native stack against CPython's frames on the heap, and the 4 to 8 KiB a level of a prelude's walk against the 100 to 200 bytes of CPython's C. In TODO.md under Later.
* **Done the same day.** `run_main` puts the program on a stack of the policy's size (`runtime/src/core.cpp`, `on_main_stack`): the address space is reserved with no memory behind it, a megabyte below it is never readable, and the main thread is switched onto it, so the thread the program runs on is still the process's first (`threading.get_native_id() == os.getpid()`). The stack watch of RL-624 measures the new stack, and the old one again once the program has returned. Going back keeps the signal mask the program left (a context switch restores the one it saved: a SIGINT the program had blocked and left pending was delivered on the way out, which `test_signals.py` caught). The build reads `[platform] main_stack_mib` from the program's nearest `bella.toml` (256 where there is none) and the main unit hands it over; the repository's own policy says 256 now.
* **The process's hard limit is kept.** The size is no more than `ulimit -Hs` allows, and where that leaves no more than the stack the process already has, the program stays on it. A plain `ulimit -s 1024` in a shell sets both limits, so a program started that way has 1 MiB as before (C-8-20 runs that way and is unchanged); a soft limit alone, which is every shell's default of 8 MiB, does not hold the program back. Without this rule nothing could run a compiled program on a small stack but a policy file. A sanitizer build stays on the first stack.
* **Measured.** A recursion under a limit of 50 000 reaches 49 998 as under CPython (11 899 on 8 MiB); under a limit of 1 000 000 it reaches 389 876 (CPython 999 998); `json.dumps` and `repr` of a list nested 5 000 deep work (RecursionError before); `json.dumps` nested 50 000 deep is still a RecursionError, `repr` works. The start-up step is one `mmap`, one `mprotect` and two context switches. A program that recurses 390 000 deep has 366 MB resident. Seen beside it and not this step's: `repr` of a list nested 50 000 deep takes 73 s compiled and 1 s under CPython (quadratic on both sides).
* **The words** of section 8's Resources paragraph, D-17's row and decision 16's row are in the specification as v0.28, for the author to check. Case C-8-21 (compiled; an evaluator's own frames count against the limit, so its numbers are others).

**2026-11-22 — a generator expression holds its iterable (RL-488 of review S22, measured again with A35; case C-3-81; no decision of the author's was needed).** CPython 3.15 gives a generator expression's code the iterable of its first `for`, which stays the code's variable `.0` for as long as the generator has its frame, and the code calls `GET_ITER` itself before the generator is made: the iterator is a value of the frame's stack. The compiled expression was given the iterator as `.0` and nothing else.
* **What a program saw.** A temporary iterable went as soon as `iter()` had returned (`sum(1 for v in Source())`: `drop iterable` before the first `next`, at the generator's end under CPython), so an iterator that needs its source alive lost it; `locals()` in the expression showed a `list_iterator` for `.0`; and the iterator went after the last item at the end, and after the iterable when a generator was given up early.
* **The lowering.** The helper `<genexpr>` takes two parameters: the iterable, shown as `.0`, and the iterator under a name of the compiler's own, which the outermost loop takes out of its variable when it starts (`resolve.moved_on_use`, the mechanism a decorator's value has). So the loop's end lets go of the iterator before the variables, a generator given up before its first `next` lets go of the iterator and then the iterable, and one given up later the iterator, the item, the iterable: CPython's order in each. The intrinsic `genexpr(helper, iterable)` calls `iter()` and the helper; a failing `iter()` has no frame of the expression in its traceback, as under CPython (the frame is not complete yet).
* **Not done, and why.** The iterator of a list, set or dict comprehension goes after the comprehension's last item compiled and before it under CPython (`stop, drop 2, drop iterator` for `stop, drop iterator, drop 2`). The helper's caller holds the iterator for the length of the call: that is the question of a call's arguments (RL-695, the author's), and the row is recorded there.
* **Checks.** C-3-81: 17 rows with objects that say when they go and a weak reference to a source only the generator holds; it passes compiled and under eval2 (eval1 does not model `locals()` in a function).

**2026-11-22 — `_math_integer` is importable by its own name (the sweep of the manifest's modules; `pipeline/tests/test_math_integer_name.py`).** The sweep run for the author's question about the next milestone compared the public names of each of the manifest's 118 phase-1 modules, CPython against compiled: 103 agree (101 on 2026-11-13). Of the 15 others, five differ as decided (D-20: `datetime`, `decimal`, `hashlib`, `hmac`, `secrets`), five stop as decided (the four listings of the import system's two modules, `unittest`'s asyncio test case), `builtins` and `sys` stop at names that are decided (`aiter`, `anext`: async is \[P2+\]) or not provided yet (50 of `sys`), and three could not be imported at all: `_math_integer`, `marshal`, `zipimport`.
* **`_math_integer`.** CPython 3.15 has the module `math.integer` as a built-in module named `_math_integer`: its spec says `_math_integer`, the module itself `math.integer`, and `math` imports it by the first name when it is made and puts it in `sys.modules` under the second as well. The runtime had it under `math.integer` alone, so `import _math_integer` stopped and `sys.modules` lacked the name after `import math`. The registry has it under `_math_integer` now, the module names itself, and `math` imports it through the import system. Six rows, one program each (what a row imports first is what it is about). The sweep's row agrees: 104 of 118.
* **Left:** `marshal` and `zipimport` (TODO.md).

**2026-11-22 — review A39 (Codex Astra, low effort, of `7f7c267..2996062`; the first review of several steps at once).** 11 findings (RL-875 to RL-885), each run on both sides, all confirmed; nine fixed, two are the stack decision's own consequences. Nothing was found on the order of a pattern's stores (66 rows matched) or on FileIO and the EINTR test under real signals (86 rows). No decision of the author's was needed; one wording is put to the author.
* **`__set__` and `__delete__` are methods of the descriptor; `__get__` is not (RL-878, high; RL-877; RL-879).** The class of RL-859 in three more places. CPython's `slot_tp_descr_set` looks the name up as a method: a function gets the descriptor first, anything else is bound by its own `__get__` (`__set__ = staticmethod(f)` calls `f(obj, value)`; the header called it with three). `slot_tp_descr_get` does not: it calls what the type has under `__get__` as it is. My first version bound `__get__` too, and a row of the reviewer's that had agreed showed the mistake. The rule is one function now (`call_as_method`), which `call_special`, the metaclass's descriptors and the header (through a hook, `call_method`) use. A prelude's built-in function stored as `__call__`, `__enter__` or `__exit__` is no longer given the object, and `classmethod` binds what is not callable (`PyMethod_New` checks nothing: the call fails, which `operator.length_hint` and a finaliser's report depend on). Swept: 17 kinds of thing under each of the three descriptor names, reached through an instance, the class and a metaclass: 153 rows agree.
* **A generator expression, five things (RL-875, RL-876, RL-880, RL-881, RL-882).** An exception thrown into one that has not started lets go of the iterator as the frame unwinds: the iterator's parameter is a stack value for unwinding (of RL-488's lowering). After PEP 479's translation the frame's variables live while the RuntimeError's cause does, whose traceback has the frame (older). The frame is at the line of the clause being asked and at the element's line when resumed, not at the expression's first line (older). A zero-argument `super()` takes the expression's `.0`, as in any function: exact only since RL-488 made `.0` the iterable. And a comprehension in the body of an `if`, a loop or a `try` has its helper made there, not before the statement: it was made whether the branch ran or not and held the cells it closes over from then on (older).
* **The stack (RL-883, RL-884, RL-885).** A `main_stack_mib` of 2**44 became no stack at all (the shift to bytes): the policy takes 1 to 2**20 MiB and the build refuses anything else. The other two are the decision seen from both sides and no defect: a recursion through `lru_cache` or `partial` 20 000 deep succeeds compiled and is a RecursionError under CPython, whose C uses its 8 MiB stack there; and on a small stack (a hard limit, or a policy of 1, 2 or 16 MiB) a compiled program stops where CPython's heap frames go on. D-17 says CPython reaches the bound "later or not at all": "earlier" belongs there too, which is the author's wording.
* **The saved outputs, pairwise.** 127 rows differed in the worktree; 26 agree now and the seven traceback rows agree on frames and lines. The others: 60 of RL-883 and RL-884, 14 stops by name, two suites refused at build (RL-885), 13 that differ in a report's text (D-28), three on resident memory the reviewer did not count.

**2026-11-22 — a slot named twice is two cells for the size test (RL-591 of review A25; `pipeline/tests/test_repeated_slot_names.py`).** CPython makes a cell for each entry of `__slots__`; the first cell of a repeated name is unreachable, and the default pickling state refuses an object larger than its `__slotnames__` account for (`TypeError: cannot pickle`). The class keeps the count of repeated entries and the size test adds them along the MRO. With `copyreg`'s own names nothing changes (it lists a name for each entry); a class that sets `__slotnames__` shorter is refused as under CPython. 19 rows.

**2026-11-22 — how a Struct gets its format (RL-549 of review A23, RL-776 of A34; case C-7-92; no decision of the author's was needed).** 3.15 lets the format reach `Struct.__new__` or, for a class with an `__init__` of its own, `Struct.__init__`, and warns where the two disagree with what a later version will allow. The prelude had the first way alone: it stopped by name for any class over `struct.Struct` and for a second format, and returned None for an `__init__` without one.
* **The port.** `s_new`, `Struct_impl`, `s_init`, `Struct___init___impl`, `set_format` and `same_format` as they are. `__new__` called through a subclass's own `__new__` takes exactly one format (the clinic's rule); called as the type's own, it takes a format or none, and for a class with its own `__init__` gives a DeprecationWarning where the format is missing, one of several arguments, or invalid (the error becomes the warning's text, given with nothing pending). `__init__` after a `__new__` that did the work only notes that it ran; called again it compares the format with the object's and, for another, gives a FutureWarning and takes it. The two tests are the slots' (`_tp_new_is`, `_tp_init_is`).
* **A Struct with no format** has no `format` (the member is `T_OBJECT_EX`), a size of -1, and methods that raise RuntimeError where `ENSURE_STRUCT_IS_READY` stands: after the buffer and the offset are converted for `unpack`, `unpack_from` and `pack_into` (the clinic's code runs first), before anything for `pack` and `iter_unpack`.
* **A class over Struct.** The methods read the struct's own fields (`_bella_state_get`), so a class that has `size` or `format` of its own under those names changes what its instances show and not what `pack` does.
* **Checks.** C-7-92 on all three legs; 59 rows with the warnings' texts on both sides (`pipeline/tests/test_struct_new_and_init.py`); the two rows of `test_system_modules.py` that held the stops are gone.

**2026-11-22 — a view's hold on its exporter, in the model first (the minimal memoryview, decided 2026-11-14; part 1 of the step).** `refmodel/objects.py` has CPython's account of it ("Buffers"): a memoryview of an object holds a managed buffer, the one hold on the object's octets, which counts as one of its exports and holds the object; every view made of that view, a slice too, shares it; the hold ends when the last view registered on it is released or dies, and then the count goes down and the object is let go of, so it can die there. The model has the managed buffer as an object that only unreleased views hold, so its death is the master's release: CPython's lives on until the last view that refers to it is gone, which nothing a program sees can tell apart.
* **Checked against CPython.** The oracle world has exporters (a bytearray and a bytes subclass whose deaths are logged) and views, behind a world option the header's lockstep test leaves off: make a view of an exporter, of a view, a slice; release one; drop one; drop the exporter; grow and shrink a bytearray. The self-test runs them on eight seeds and the Hypothesis machine has them as rules. Four wrong models are caught (no refusal of a resize, no export counted, a release that holds on, a released view that makes views); a view of a view that takes a hold of its own on the exporter is not, and cannot be: with the counts only compared to nought, the two are the same to a program.

**2026-11-22 — review A40 processed (RL-886 to RL-903): the memoryview's checks against the program's code, and two stops.** All 18 confirmed; 16 fixed to CPython's code and two by a stop by name. **2026-11-23 — the collector in the model by trial deletion (the author's decision of 2026-11-14; step 3c, the model first).** `refmodel/collector.py` is rewritten: a release that leaves a tracked object above nought buffers it as a candidate root (`GcState.possible_root`, from `decref` and from a finaliser's own count released after it revived its object); a collection takes the buffer's roots through `mark_gray`, `scan` and `collect_white`, and from the unreachable list on keeps gc.c's 3.15 sequence, whose reckoning again (`deduce_unreachable`) now runs over the collection's own list only. No generations: `GcState` keeps the tracked objects, the buffer, the thresholds (the first the buffer's size, decided the same day), the objects tracked after the last collection (the quarter rule taken that day), and the runtime's own counters (`count()`: the candidates and two noughts; collections and collected for `get_stats`).
* **Checked.** The oracle's collection is a full one on CPython's side too (its young collections would leave what the model's full one finds); eight seeds of the self-test agree, and the Hypothesis machine's runs pass. Three wrong collectors are caught (no roots, half the roots, everything reached taken). The fourth, no candidate for an object a finaliser revives into a cycle of its own, the worlds never make, so `test_an_object_revived_into_a_cycle_of_its_own_is_a_candidate` holds it against CPython. The generation tests are replaced by the decision's: every collection is full, and the buffer schedules a collection by the threshold and the quarter rule.
* **Next:** the header (`object.hpp`) from the model, `make object-test` with `World(cycles=True)`, then `gc.collect` and the exit walk.

**2026-11-23 — the collector in the header, part 1 (H1 of TODO's plan): the object model's kinds, in lockstep.** `object.hpp` has the collector of `refmodel/collector.py`: the buffer (BUFFERED), the candidates a release above nought makes, the release of a finaliser's own count after a revival included (the header's and the generator's resurrection paths), trial deletion in the model's order and gc.c's sequence after it. Two mechanisms of the header's own, which nothing a program sees can tell: an object that dies while buffered is destroyed then and there, its destructor run as any other's, and its header is left as a TOMBSTONE that the next walk of the buffer frees (the header has no room for its place in the buffer; a compaction runs once half the buffer is tombstones); and a running collection's lists name their objects in a side table (LISTED), which an object leaves as it dies. The traversals and clears are those of the object model's kinds and of the base data they carry; the runtime's kinds register theirs in part 2, and until then a group through one of them is kept, never freed early.
* **Found by the lockstep, fixed from the model:** after a collection's clear, the model leaves an exposed instance with no dict and no values; the header left it marked exposed, so its death read a dict that was gone (ASan).
* **Checked.** The binding gets the oracle's `ring` and `collect` and the program's objects as values, and its tagged classes log in a finaliser as the oracle's do (a collection finalises a group a reviver keeps, and CPython logs it). `object_differential.py --cycles`: six seeds agree, two more under ASan and UBSan; a collector with no roots and a traversal that counts a slot twice are caught; `make object-test` runs both lockstep kinds. Nothing calls the collector yet (part 3).

**2026-11-23 — the collector in the header, parts 2 and 3 (H2, H3): the runtime's kinds and the gc module.** Each kind of the runtime registers its traverse and its clear, written from CPython's `tp_traverse` and `tp_clear` of its counterpart and from what its own destroyer releases: a cell, a function (its globals, `__module__`, defaults, its dict, its closure and the tuple of it, the annotations, its names; its clear is func_clear's and leaves its globals, which the program's modules are), a built-in function and a bound method, a staged text's module, the iterators and dict views, a lazy import, super, a traceback (the next entry, then the frame: its module, its locals, a class body's namespace), a frame, a slice, a mappingproxy, the abc data, and a generator (its names, then its frame while it has one: the function, its slots, what it was sent or thrown, its delegate, the exceptions its handlers caught; then the exceptions it keeps while suspended). A kind CPython gives no `tp_clear` has none, and a cycle through it is broken by clearing another member.
* **The gc module over the collector.** `gc.collect()` runs a collection with `gc.callbacks` before and after it (their info in 3.15's five keys), none while one runs, and stops by name when a debugging flag is set (`gc.set_debug`); `get_count()` and `get_stats()` give the runtime's own numbers in CPython's shapes (3.15's five keys); `is_finalized()` reads the flag the finaliser sets; `enable`, `disable` and the thresholds are the collector's own. `is_tracked()` stays a stop: CPython untracks tuples and dicts by rules of its own, to be measured first. A collection the buffer schedules sets a bit of the runtime's breaker word, and the look runs it after the signals and the pending calls, as `_Py_HandlePending` does; a lean build's looks are the runtime's own (D-24).
* **What it ends.** Five P1 cases pass compiled now and join the baseline (C-9-01, C-9-02, C-9-07, C-7-06, C-7-11); of the cases outside it, C-3-12 waits for classes as counted objects (a class is immortal in the runtime), and C-9-11 for part 4 (RL-222). D-04's note gets the collector's observables. A sweep of cycles through every kind agrees with CPython compiled (`pipeline/tests/test_cycle_collector.py`).
* **Measured, for later:** with collections on, a loop that keeps 2,000,000 new lists takes 4.4 s compiled against 1.4 s with them off (CPython 0.9 s): each collection walks the kept list from it, the quarter rule spacing the walks geometrically, and the trial counts and colours are side tables. Trial counts in the objects' own counts (restored after) and colours in the header's free bits are the step that remains; TODO.

A collection's guard covers its callbacks; a kind's own tp_finalize (a generator's close) runs in the collection's pass; the release path marks an object finalised after its finaliser, the pass before; the debugging flags are read where gc.c reads them (DEBUG_SAVEALL saves to gc.garbage; DEBUG_STATS and DEBUG_COLLECTABLE, whose lines are CPython's own counts and addresses, stop by name). Two classes, each settled for all its members: the pass runs a prelude `__del__` only where CPython's type has a tp_finalize (the names table records it as `__del__`), and the helpers whose `__del__` is C dealloc code say `__bella_dealloc__`, which a source rule checks; and a prelude class stands for its C type to the collector, untracked where the type has no Py_TPFLAGS_HAVE_GC (from the names table, nine types) and leaving the fields its tp_traverse does not visit (`__untraversed__`, the regex scanner's subject). The comparison of the prelude's slots with the C traverse functions covered thirteen modules; the scanner's was the one field CPython holds and does not visit.

**2026-11-23 — a buffer argument held from its parsing to the call's end (RL-702, RL-583, the class).** CPython's clinic takes a Py_buffer at the argument's parsing and lets it go at the call's exit, and the work reads through it; the preludes copied the octets at the conversion. Now every prelude function with a buffer argument and a later converted argument holds it (`_held`: the converter's checks, then an export, by the runtime's `_buffer_export`: a bytearray's count, a memoryview's own) and reads it where the C reads it, letting it go in a `finally` after the work's own clean-up: zlib's compress, decompress, crc32, adler32 and both decompressors, binascii's crc32, hexlify and the base64 and base32 coders, struct's unpack_from in both forms, blake2's key, salt and person, os.pwrite. So a later argument's code sees its write to a bytearray used, cannot resize it, and cannot release the view it came from. `struct.iter_unpack`'s iterator keeps its buffer in a private hold until it ends or goes, released after the Struct, and reads each step's octets as they are then (RL-583). A function whose buffer is its last converted argument had nothing to run in between and is unchanged. A sweep of the class (39 rows) agrees with CPython.

**2026-11-23 — the nightly's disk.** The night of 2026-11-23 (on `540dabc`) failed with "No space left on device" on the runner, not on a test: `make checks` keeps a build directory per compiled program inside the container, and the day's new tests (reviews A40 and A41, the collector's) took the run past the hosted runner's free space. The nightly job now removes the hosted image's own toolchains, which it never uses (everything runs in the pinned image), before it builds, and prints `df` before, after and at the end. If that is not room enough, the next step is to free each program's directory once its run is over.

**2026-11-23 — a frame's values outlive it while its frame object is held (RL-222; D-12, part 2, decided 2026-11-03 to come with the collector).** CPython gives a frame one frame object at most, made at the first asking (its first traceback entry); the entries share it as `tb_frame`, and when the frame is over while something else holds the object, the object takes the frame's values and, as `f_back`, the frame object of its caller, which it makes if there is none (`_PyFrame_ClearExceptCode`, `take_ownership`, Python/frame.c 3.15). So a function that catches an exception and returns it keeps its variables while the exception lives, and so does the function that called it, whose own end finds its frame object held by `f_back`; measured under 3.15.2 before writing it. The runtime gave the locals to the exception's own entry, and only when the exception left the frame. Now each frame record holds its frame object once one is made (`FrameRecord::frame_obj`), each entry holds it (`Traceback::frame_obj`), and the frame's end (`detail::frame_ends`, from the guard) lets it die first when nothing else holds it, or has it take the values, the class body's namespace and `f_back`. A generator keeps its one frame across resumes (`Generator::resumed`: the frame's id and frame object; the emitter's `frame.resume`), hands it on at a yield, and at its end, at a close or at its dealloc clears it as `gen_clear_frame` does: a frame object held elsewhere then takes the slots, its stack values with them, without `f_back`. The frame object's dealloc, traverse and clear are CPython's (`frame_dealloc`: the namespace, the values last first, then `f_back`; `frame_traverse`; `frame_tp_clear`: the values last first, then the namespace, `f_back` staying), and the entry's are `tb_dealloc`, `tb_traverse` and `tb_clear`, so a frame object in a cycle (an exception kept in a local of its own frame) is the collector's. The first design, the values in the newest live entry and handed on as entries died, was dropped before any commit: the collector could not see the values from the frame's other entries, so it could free what one of them still kept. Also from it: a bare `raise` after a `yield` adds no second entry of the generator's frame (the frame's id is one across resumes); traceback's suggestion reads the frame object of a frame that is over (a frame that caught and returned, a class body's, the first of two exceptions), and stops by name only for a generator's frame while it waits; `traceback.clear_frames` releases a returned frame's values and namespace; the unraisable report's entry of the running frame is made as `traceback_from_current_frame` makes it. No cost measured: a loop of two million raises caught in a called function takes 15.4 s against 15.6 s before (CPython 0.7 s; the throws, TODO). C-9-11 passes compiled and joins the baseline, and C-9-12 with it (a caller kept through `f_back`, a frame an exception passed, a generator that kept an exception in its first resume, one closed while suspended in a loop, a bare `raise` after a `yield`); `pipeline/tests/test_kept_frames.py` has the rows.

Two are of RL-222's step: when a generator's frame ends while its frame object is held, the object takes the values without `f_back` (`clear_gen_frame` sets the frame's previous to NULL first; the runtime linked the last resumer, whose variables then lived as long as the kept exception: RL-912), and a frame object's clear (`traceback.clear_frames`, the collection's) releases its slots in place from the top, so a finaliser one of them runs finds those below and a clear it starts releases them (RL-917). Eight are of the held buffers: blake2's holds inside the clean-up's `try` (RL-913); `a2b_base64` and `a2b_base32` in the clinic's order, `ignorechars` held, the alphabet's table built after the conversions and the octets read after its lookup (RL-914, RL-915, RL-919, RL-920); an exhausted `iter_unpack` iterator lets go of its Struct before its buffer (RL-918); `hexlify`'s separator read from what the object stores (RL-921), and the runtime's `bytes.hex` and `bytearray.hex` the same way, a wide kind refused and a NUL separator written (RL-922). The model has no prelude helpers, so the header's change has no counterpart there.
